-- 1. Grab and normalize the installation root path
local installPath = AshitaCore:GetInstallPath()
if not string.match(installPath, "[/\\]$") then
    installPath = installPath .. "/"
end

-- 2. Build the cross-platform path (Forward slashes work on Windows and Linux/Wine)
-- NOTE: Ensure 'luAshitacast' and 'MobDB' match your folder casing exactly!
local path = string.format('%sconfig/addons/luAshitacast/common/MobDB/%u.lua', installPath, curr)

-- 3. Check, clear, and hot-swap the definitions
if ashita.fs.exists(path) then
    local chunk, loadError = loadfile(path)

    if chunk then
        -- Clear the table *in-place* to preserve memory references across your addon
        for k in pairs(AreaTable) do
            AreaTable[k] = nil
        end

        -- Execute the file and populate the table with the new rows
        local freshData = chunk()
        for k, v in pairs(freshData) do
            AreaTable[k] = v
        end
    else
        print(string.format("[MobDB Error] Syntax error in file %u.lua: %s", curr, tostring(loadError)))
    end
else
    -- Optional fallback handling if the specific mob file doesn't exist
    print(string.format("[MobDB Warning] No specific definition file found for ID: %u", curr))
end

-- Define your central reference table globally or at the top of the file
AreaTable = AreaTable or {}

-- =============================================================================
-- 1. THE CORE REUSABLE LOAD FUNCTION
-- =============================================================================
local function loadZoneData(zoneId)
    if not zoneId or zoneId == 0 then return false end

    -- Normalize paths for cross-platform compatibility (Windows & Linux/Wine)
    local installPath = AshitaCore:GetInstallPath()
    if not string.match(installPath, "[/\\]$") then
        installPath = installPath .. "/"
    end

    local path = string.format('%sconfig/addons/luAshitacast/common/MobDB/%u.lua', installPath, zoneId)

    if ashita.fs.exists(path) then
        local chunk, loadError = loadfile(path)
        if chunk then
            -- Wipe old data in-place to preserve references
            for k in pairs(AreaTable) do AreaTable[k] = nil end

            -- Inject fresh definitions
            local freshData = chunk()
            for k, v in pairs(freshData) do AreaTable[k] = v end

            print(string.format("[MobDB] Successfully loaded definitions for Zone %u.", zoneId))
            return true
        else
            print(string.format("[MobDB Error] Syntax error in file %u.lua: %s", zoneId, tostring(loadError)))
        end
    else
        -- Wipe the table if a zone doesn't have a file so old data doesn't bleed over
        for k in pairs(AreaTable) do AreaTable[k] = nil end
        print(string.format("[MobDB] No data file for Zone %u. Table cleared.", zoneId))
    end

    return false
end

-- =============================================================================
-- 2. AUTOMATIC LOADING AT STARTUP / LOGIN
-- =============================================================================
ashita.events.register('load', 'load_callback1', function ()
    -- Wait a frame or check if the memory manager is ready
    local partyMgr = AshitaCore:GetMemoryManager():GetParty()
    if partyMgr then
        local currentZoneId = partyMgr:GetMemberZone(0) -- 0 is the local player
        if currentZoneId and currentZoneId ~= 0 then
            print(string.format("[MobDB] Initialized! Auto-loading current Zone %u...", currentZoneId))
            loadZoneData(currentZoneId)
        end
    end
end)

-- =============================================================================
-- 3. AUTOMATIC LOADING WHEN ZONING (PACKETS)
-- =============================================================================
ashita.events.register('packet_in', 'packet_in_callback1', function (e)
    if (e.id == 0x05E) then
        -- Your existing read-only regional conquest logic goes here

    elseif (e.id == 0x000A) then
        -- Grab the new Zone ID from byte 49 using e.data
        local newZoneId = struct.unpack('H', e.data, 49)
        loadZoneData(newZoneId)
    end

    e.blocked = false
end)

-- =============================================================================
-- 4. MANUAL RELOAD VIA SLASH COMMAND (e.g., /mobdb reload)
-- =============================================================================
ashita.events.register('command', 'command_callback1', function (e)
    -- Parse the command args
    local args = e.command:args()
    if #args == 0 then return false end

    -- Pick a slash command name for your addon (e.g., /mobdb or /mdb)
    if args[1]:lower() == '/mobdb' or args[1]:lower() == '/mdb' then

        -- Check if they typed 'reload' (e.g., /mobdb reload)
        if #args >= 2 and args[2]:lower() == 'reload' then
            local partyMgr = AshitaCore:GetMemoryManager():GetParty()
            if partyMgr then
                local currentZoneId = partyMgr:GetMemberZone(0)
                print("[MobDB] Force-reloading zone definitions from disk...")

                if loadZoneData(currentZoneId) then
                    print("[MobDB] Force-reload complete!")
                else
                    print("[MobDB] Force-reload finished (No file or file failed to load).")
                end
            else
                print("[MobDB Error] Cannot reload: Memory manager not ready.")
            end

            -- Tell Ashita we handled this command so it doesn't pass a "Command not found" to FFXI
            e.blocked = true
        end
    end

    return false
end)


To handle extreme disconnect events without leaving your UI stranded, you have to look at how Ashita itself behaves during crashes and network failures.
    1. Detecting a Full Game Client Crash
    If the FFXI client itself crashes (e.g., a hard desktop crash or a Direct3D crash), your Lua environment crashes right along with it. In that scenario,
    you do not need to write code to reset isDownloadingData because the entire addon process is instantly terminated. When the player boots the game back up,
    your script will reload completely from scratch, initializing isDownloadingData = false naturally.

    2. Detecting a Network Disconnect (R0 or Kick to Title Screen)
    If a player gets disconnected from the server due to network issues (the dreaded Red "0" or an error code like 3101), the game client does not close, but
    the network link is severed. Because your packet_in event depends on a healthy connection, it will stop receiving server data entirely during an R0 disconnect.
    To prevent isDownloadingData from getting locked onto true permanently if they drop out mid-zone, you can implement two robust safety layers:

    Safety Layer A: The Global Status Check (Recommended)Instead of relying strictly on network packets to track the loading screen state, you can cross-reference
    Ashita's native Status Pointer. When a player is disconnected, logs out, or is kicked back to the POL/Title screen, their character status index shifts.

    You can add a fast status validation directly inside your d3d_present UI loop:

-- Inside your regular UI / Render tick
ashita.events.register('d3d_present', 'present_callback1', function ()
    -- 1. Grab the status pointer
    local status = AshitaCore:GetMemoryManager():GetPlayer():GetStatus()

    -- 2. If status is invalid, or 0 (when completely unlogged/disconnected)
    -- reset the flag so the UI resets cleanly.
    if not status or status == 0 then
        isDownloadingData = false
        return -- Hide the UI because the player is effectively gone
    end

    -- 3. Your loading screen guard clause
    if isDownloadingData then
        return
    end

    -- Draw your job UI safely here...
end)


    Safety Layer B: Logging Out Gracefully
    If a player triggers a normal logout or a soft disconnect that brings them back to the main character select screen, Ashita exposes an event hook specifically for
    cleanup. You can register the 'unload' or 'logout' event hooks to instantly catch when an addon is unceremoniously shut down or logged out, purging the data:

-- Fires if a player manually types /logout or gets pushed back to character select
ashita.events.register('logout', 'logout_callback1', function()
    isDownloadingData = false
    -- Clear the table so no ghost data stays in memory
    for k in pairs(AreaTable) do AreaTable[k] = nil end
end)

Summary of the perfect workflow:
    By linking 0x000A (enter), 0x000B (exit), logout (network drop/exit), and utilizing a simple GetStatus() memory check, your addon becomes practically un-breakable.
    It will handle lightning-fast zone transitions perfectly, and handle catastrophic network drops gracefully without breaking your UI.


---

To build a perfect visual overlay that matches atom0s' equipmon grid, you can leverage Dear ImGui's drawing capabilities in Ashita v4. Because equipmon renders its equipment
icons in a very distinct 4x4 square grid layout, you can read its current position directly from the settings or track its bounding boxes to place your visual indicators
perfectly on top.

Combining your custom locking system table with a native game client check provides two distinct styles of visual indicators to separate your automated scripting locks from
natural in-game locks (such as a two-handed weapon disabling your sub/grip slot).

    Step 1: Mapping the Grid LayoutFirst, ensure your custom lock table uses indices that match equipmon's grid rendering layout. equipmon draws 16 slots. We will establish
    two tables: your existing custom lock tracker and a second state table to catch native engine locks.

    -- Global or top-of-addon file variables
    MyCustomLocks = MyCustomLocks or {} -- true if your script locked it
    NativeGearLocks = NativeGearLocks or {} -- true if the game client locked it (e.g. 2-Handed weapon)

    -- Map slot IDs to their names for easy reference
    local slot_names = {
        [0] = 'Main',  [1] = 'Sub',   [2] = 'Range', [3] = 'Ammo',
        [4] = 'Head',  [5] = 'Body',  [6] = 'Hands', [7] = 'Legs',
        [8] = 'Feet',  [9] = 'Neck',  [10] = 'Waist',[11] = 'Ear1',
        [12] = 'Ear2', [13] = 'Ring1', [14] = 'Ring2',[15] = 'Back'
    }

    -- Equipmon renders in an exact 4x4 matrix, but the display index order
    -- doesn't always map 0 to 15 sequentially. To line up your boxes perfectly with
    -- atom0s' grid layout, we map grid position (0 to 15) to their FFXI equipment slot IDs:
    local grid_to_slot = {
        [0]  = 0,  -- Main  (Top Row, Box 1)
        [1]  = 1,  -- Sub   (Top Row, Box 2)
        [2]  = 2,  -- Range (Top Row, Box 3)
        [3]  = 3,  -- Ammo  (Top Row, Box 4)
        [4]  = 4,  -- Head  (Second Row, Box 1)
        [5]  = 9,  -- Neck  (Second Row, Box 2)
        [6]  = 11, -- Ear1  (Second Row, Box 3)
        [7]  = 12, -- Ear2  (Second Row, Box 4)
        [8]  = 5,  -- Body  (Third Row, Box 1)
        [9]  = 6,  -- Hands (Third Row, Box 2)
        [10] = 13, -- Ring1 (Third Row, Box 3)
        [11] = 14, -- Ring2 (Third Row, Box 4)
        [12] = 15, -- Back  (Bottom Row, Box 1)
        [13] = 10, -- Waist (Bottom Row, Box 2)
        [14] = 7,  -- Legs  (Bottom Row, Box 3)
        [15] = 8   -- Feet  (Bottom Row, Box 4)
    }

    Step 2: Grabbing Native In-Game Locks

    To fulfill your second requirement (differentiating your locks from game locks), you can query the FFXI memory block to see if the engine itself has restricted a slot.
    The most common instance of this is an equipped two-handed weapon locking out the Sub slot.

    We can track this inside your data polling function:

    local function checkNativeLocks()
        local inventory = AshitaCore:GetMemoryManager():GetInventory()
        if not inventory then return end

        for slot_id = 0, 15 do
            -- Clear state
            NativeGearLocks[slot_id] = false

            -- Ashita's native container tracker flags "encumbered" or blocked slots
            -- If a 2-Handed weapon is held, slot_id 1 (Sub) will return true here.
            if inventory:IsSlotLocked(slot_id) then
                NativeGearLocks[slot_id] = true
            end
        end
    end

    Step 3: Drawing the ImGui Overlay Window

    Now, we construct a 100% transparent click-through window positioned directly over the top of equipmon. We will use colored text characters or stylized border symbols to
    cue the player.

    Custom script locks will show an aggressive [X] in bright red.

    Native gear locks (like 2-handed weapons blocking the grip slot) will display a yellow [L] (Locked).

    local imgui = require('imgui')

    ashita.events.register('d3d_present', 'present_callback_overlay', function ()
        -- 1. Run your network/memory status checks first
        checkNativeLocks()

        -- 2. Build the borderless window wrapper
        imgui.set_next_window_bg_alpha(0.0) -- Makes the window completely transparent

        -- IMPORTANT: WindowFlags_NoInputs makes the overlay click-through so it never
        -- blocks your mouse or interferes with equipmon/the game screen.
        local flags = imgui.WindowFlags_NoDecoration
                    + imgui.WindowFlags_AlwaysAutoResize
                    + imgui.WindowFlags_NoInputs
                    + imgui.WindowFlags_NoNav

        -- Open the window frame
        if imgui.begin('EquipmonLockOverlay', true, flags) then

            -- 3. Loop through 0 to 15 to form the exact 4x4 layout grid matrix
            for grid_idx = 0, 15 do
                local slot_id = grid_to_slot[grid_idx]

                -- Evaluate Priorities:
                if MyCustomLocks[slot_id] then
                    -- Render a bright Red indication for your manual script locks
                    imgui.text_colored({1.0, 0.1, 0.1, 1.0}, ' [X] ')

                elseif NativeGearLocks[slot_id] then
                    -- Render a Gold/Yellow indication for client-enforced gear locks
                    imgui.text_colored({1.0, 0.8, 0.2, 1.0}, ' [L] ')

                else
                    -- Render an empty placeholder spacing block so the alignment
                    -- of the remaining grid squares doesn't collapse!
                    imgui.text_colored({1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 0.0}, ' [ ] ') -- Alpha 0.0 = completely invisible
                end

                -- ImGui builds left-to-right. We force everything onto the same visual line
                -- EXCEPT when we hit the end of a 4-item row grid boundary.
                if (grid_idx + 1) % 4 ~= 0 then
                    imgui.same_line()
                end
            end

            imgui.end()
        end
    end)

    Step 4: Aligning it perfectly with Equipmon

    Because players move their UI elements around, hardcoding the position will break if they slide equipmon
    somewhere else on the screen. To make the overlay track equipmon seamlessly, you have two choices:

    The Shared Font/Padding Hack (Easiest): Open your overlay's ImGui menu once, turn off WindowFlags_NoInputs
    temporarily, drag your custom [X] box directly on top of equipmon, and let ImGui save the position to your
    local layout cache file. Once it is perfectly aligned, toggle WindowFlags_NoInputs back on so it locks into
    place permanently as a click-through widget.

    Reading Equipmon Settings Directly: You can parse equipmon's settings file (config/addons/equipmon/settings.json)
    at startup to read its X and Y pixel coordinates. Then, inside your render tick, inject
    imgui.set_next_window_pos(equipmon_x, equipmon_y) right before calling imgui.begin to make your lock layer magnetize
    directly to atom0s' interface framework.

    ---

    In Ashita v4, you can detect if another addon or plugin is running.

    Because equipmon is distributed exclusively as an Ashita Plugin (a compiled .dll file) rather than an addon
    (a collection of script .lua files), you can query Ashita's internal manager natively to check its state.

    How to Detect equipmon Natively

    Ashita v4 exposes the C++ plugin manager straight to Lua via AshitaCore:GetPluginManager(). You can check if
    equipmon is active in memory using its plugin name:

    local function isEquipmonLoaded()
        local pluginManager = AshitaCore:GetPluginManager()
        if not pluginManager then return false end

        -- Query the plugin manager directly for atom0s' plugin name
        -- This handles the case if a player typed '/unload equipmon' mid-session
        return pluginManager:IsLoaded('equipmon')
    end

    Making the Lock Grid Completely Optional

    You can blend this check with a config setting. This ensures that even if equipmon is loaded, a player who
    prefers a minimal UI can still disable your lock grid overlay completely.

    Here is how you adjust the logic inside your isolated lock_grid.lua file to handle this state checking dynamically:

    local imgui = require('imgui')

    -- Player configurations (Could be pulled from your main settings file)
    local show_lock_grid_overlay = true

    -- Reusable detection helper
    local function shouldRenderGrid()
        -- Guard 1: Did the player explicitly turn off the grid in the settings?
        if not show_lock_grid_overlay then return false end

        -- Guard 2: Is the player currently stuck in a loading screen?
        if isDownloadingData then return false end

        -- Guard 3: Is atom0s' equipmon plugin physically loaded into the game process?
        local pluginManager = AshitaCore:GetPluginManager()
        if not pluginManager or not pluginManager:IsLoaded('equipmon') then
            return false
        end

        return true
    end

    -- Your dedicated d3d_present loop for the grid
    ashita.events.register('d3d_present', 'equipmon_lock_grid_render', function ()
        -- Fast exit if any of our optional conditions are not met
        if not shouldRenderGrid() then
            return
        end

        -- Run native memory lock checks (like 2-Handed weapon detection)
        checkNativeLocks()

        -- Render the 4x4 ImGui layer safely over equipmon
        imgui.set_next_window_bg_alpha(0.0)
        local flags = imgui.WindowFlags_NoDecoration
                    + imgui.WindowFlags_AlwaysAutoResize
                    + imgui.WindowFlags_NoInputs
                    + imgui.WindowFlags_NoNav

        if imgui.begin('EquipmonLockOverlay', true, flags) then
            for grid_idx = 0, 15 do
                local slot_id = grid_to_slot[grid_idx]

                if MyCustomLocks[slot_id] then
                    imgui.text_colored({1.0, 0.1, 0.1, 1.0}, ' [X] ')
                elseif NativeGearLocks[slot_id] then
                    imgui.text_colored({1.0, 0.8, 0.2, 1.0}, ' [L] ')
                else
                    imgui.text_colored({1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 0.0}, ' [ ] ')
                end

                if (grid_idx + 1) % 4 ~= 0 then
                    imgui.same_line()
                end
            end
            imgui.end()
        end
    end)

    Why this approach works:

    True Fail-Safe: If equipmon is absent, the execution bails out at the third statement of shouldRenderGrid().
    No phantom, empty transparent windows will generate on the player's screen, and zero computing resources are
    wasted trying to render alignment spacers.

    Synchronized State Handling: If a player logs in without equipmon, but decides to type /load equipmon later
    during their play session, the next frame will automatically detect it and instantly render your lock indicators
    right on top.

    Would you like to add a toggle configuration subcommand—such as /mobdb grid—to your 'command' event
    block so the player can switch show_lock_grid_overlay between true and false on the fly?

    By implementing this subcommand, you maintain clean encapsulation. Since your background is in software engineering,
    you will appreciate how this is wired: your main file acts as the router for the incoming user command, changes a
    state variable, and your completely decoupled lock_grid.lua immediately picks it up on the next rendering tick.

    Here is how to seamlessly add the /mobdb grid toggle command into your command routing system.

    Step 1: Initialize the Config Flag (Main File)At the top of your main script file, declare the configuration tracking
    boolean so it spans across your loaded sub-files:

    -- Global settings table or separate state flag
    ShowLockGridOverlay = true -- Enabled by default

    Step 2: Update Your Command Handler (Main File)
    In your command router block, expand your conditional evaluations to check for the 'grid' argument string.

    -- Inside your main file command callback event:
    ashita.events.register('command', 'command_callback1', function (e)
        local args = e.command:args()
        if #args == 0 then return false end

        -- Evaluate the primary slash command hook
        if args[1]:lower() == '/mobdb' or args[1]:lower() == '/mdb' then

            -- Subcommand 1: Your existing manual data reload logic
            if #args >= 2 and args[2]:lower() == 'reload' then
                local partyMgr = AshitaCore:GetMemoryManager():GetParty()
                if partyMgr then
                    loadZoneData(partyMgr:GetMemberZone(0))
                end
                e.blocked = true

            -- Subcommand 2: NEW Toggle Command for the UI overlay (/mobdb grid)
            elseif #args >= 2 and args[2]:lower() == 'grid' then
                -- Invert the state flag cleanly
                ShowLockGridOverlay = not ShowLockGridOverlay

                -- Provide localized chat feedback to the player using Ashita's logging styling
                if ShowLockGridOverlay then
                    print("[MobDB UI] Equipmon gear lock grid overlay has been enabled.")
                else
                    print("[MobDB UI] Equipmon gear lock grid overlay has been disabled.")
                end

                e.blocked = true -- Block it so the FFXI game chat engine ignores it
            end
        end

        return false
    end)

    Step 3: Wire it Into the Rendering Validation Layer (lock_grid.lua)

    Now, update the shouldRenderGrid() function inside your isolated lock_grid.lua sub-file to read the dynamic
    configuration flag:

    -- Inside lock_grid.lua
    local function shouldRenderGrid()
        -- Guard 1: Read the toggle flag controlled by our user command
        if not ShowLockGridOverlay then
            return false
        end

        -- Guard 2: Is the player actively stuck downloading area data?
        if isDownloadingData then
            return false
        end

        -- Guard 3: Is atom0s' equipmon plugin physically running?
        local pluginManager = AshitaCore:GetPluginManager()
        if not pluginManager or not pluginManager:IsLoaded('equipmon') then
            return false
        end

        return true
    end

    How the Components Execute and Cooperate:

    Decoupled execution paths: The command callback handles string manipulation and parsing independently. It never
    has to worry about structural loops, matrix spacing, or coordinate tracking.

    Instantaneous Feedback: When the user prints /mobdb grid in chat, the next D3D graphic hardware frame immediately
    intercepts the flag state shift. If turned off, it skips rendering completely, leaving equipmon perfectly unaltered.

    Resilient Boundaries: If a player tries to toggle it on while running without equipmon, it sets the flag to true,
    but your system safety filters cleanly drop it out at Guard 3 before creating any dangling or overlapping ImGui
    display anomalies.

    Transitioning your inspect tool output into an HTML file exporter is a brilliant architectural step. Since your engine
    already generates colorized tokens for the standard Ashita chat log (using internal color codes like \253 or raw hex
    markers), translating those formatting arrays into standard HTML <span style="color:#RRGGBB"> blocks allows players to
    save, catalog, and read their gear statuses directly inside any modern web browser or on a second screen.

    To implement this without blocking Ashita's primary execution frame, you can build a lightweight file-writing subroutine
    inside your command engine using standard Lua file I/O operations.

    Here is the modular implementation to append or overwrite a stylized HTML report directly onto the player's disk.

    Step 1: Create a Reusable HTML File ExporterYou can structure this as an encapsulated module or append it into your
    main core handler. It formats a clean, responsive black-background document that beautifully mirrors the dark visual
    aesthetic of an MMORPG terminal or chat log.

    local function exportGearLogToHTML(filename, characterName, gearLinesTable)
        -- 1. Resolve your absolute path using your cross-platform strategy
        local installPath = AshitaCore:GetInstallPath()
        if not string.match(installPath, "[/\\]$") then
            installPath = installPath .. "/"
        end

        -- Ensure the output directory exists or save it straight to your addon directory
        local fullPath = string.format("%sconfig/addons/your_addon/%s.html", installPath, filename)

        -- 2. Open the file handle in write mode ("w" overwrites fresh every run)
        local file, err = io.open(fullPath, "w")
        if not file then
            print("[MobDB Error] Failed to create HTML file: " .. tostring(err))
            return false
        end

        -- 3. Write standard HTML5 structural header boilerplate with modern CSS styling
        file:write("<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html>\n<head>\n")
        file:write("    <meta charset='UTF-8'>\n")
        file:write("    <title>Gear Inspection Report - " .. characterName .. "</title>\n")
        file:write("    <style>\n")
        file:write("        body { background-color: #1a1a1a; color: #ffffff; font-family: 'Courier New', monospace; padding: 20px; line-height: 1.6; }\n")
        file:write("        .container { max-width: 800px; margin: 0 auto; background-color: #111111; padding: 15px; border-radius: 5px; border: 1px solid #333; }\n")
        file:write("        h1 { color: #e6b800; border-bottom: 2px solid #333; padding-bottom: 10px; font-size: 20px; }\n")
        file:write("        .timestamp { color: #888; font-size: 12px; margin-bottom: 20px; }\n")
        file:write("        .log-line { margin: 4px 0; white-space: pre-wrap; font-size: 14px; }\n")
        file:write("    </style>\n</head>\n<body>\n")

        file:write("<div class='container'>\n")
        file:write("    <h1>Gear Accessibility Report: " .. characterName .. "</h1>\n")
        file:write("    <div class='timestamp'>Generated on: " .. os.date("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S") .. "</div>\n")

        -- 4. Process and write the tokenized data rows
        -- Assumes gearLinesTable contains strings wrapped in inline styles:
        -- e.g., "Slot Main: <span style='color:#ff3333;'>Locked</span>"
        for _, line in ipairs(gearLinesTable) do
            file:write("    <div class='log-line'>" .. line .. "</div>\n")
        end

        -- 5. Close out structural HTML tags safely
        file:write("</div>\n</body>\n</html>")

        -- 6. Close the file handle to flush buffer caches natively to disk
        file:close()
        return true
    end

    Step 2: Converting Ashita Chat Strings to HTML Spans

    Because your existing code likely extracts color designations using Ashita color codes or chat tags, you can create a simple translation loop using
    standard Lua patterns (string.gsub) before dumping the lines array into your exporter.

    If you pass structured layout strings down, you can translate them on the fly:

    local function convertChatToHTML(rawText, hexColor)
        -- Escapes raw HTML character conflicts to prevent data rendering glitches
        local cleanText = string.gsub(rawText, "&", "&amp;")
        cleanText = string.gsub(cleanText, "<", "&lt;")
        cleanText = string.gsub(cleanText, ">", "&gt;")

        -- Wraps your target string inside a standard inline color block
        return string.format("<span style='color:%s;'>%s</span>", hexColor, cleanText)
    end

    Step 3: Triggering via Optional Command Parameters

    To fulfill your requirement to make this completely optional, you can expand your command parser. If a user types /mobdb inspect, it defaults to
    printing directly to the standard in-game Ashita log. If they append a trailing parameter—like /mobdb inspect file—your routine skips the chat
    line printer and sends the array payload over to exportGearLogToHTML instead.

    -- Inside your main command block handler loop:
    if args:lower() == '/mobdb' or args:lower() == '/mdb' then
        if #args >= 2 and args:lower() == 'inspect' then

            -- Determine target output route based on optional parameter arguments
            local outputToFile = false
            if #args >= 3 and args:lower() == 'file' then
                outputToFile = true
            end

            -- Compile your dynamic gear data strings
            local reportLines = {}
            table.insert(reportLines, convertChatToHTML("Slot Main: Kraken Club ", "#00ff00") .. convertChatToHTML("[Accessible - Wardrobe 1]", "#888888"))
            table.insert(reportLines, convertChatToHTML("Slot Sub:  Grip Token ", "#ff3333") .. convertChatToHTML("[LOCKED BY 2-HANDED RULE]", "#ffaa00"))

            if outputToFile then
                local charName = AshitaCore:GetMemoryManager():GetParty():GetMemberName(0)
                local success = exportGearLogToHTML("gear_report", charName, reportLines)

                if success then
                    print("[MobDB File] Report successfully exported to your addon directory as gear_report.html!")
                end
            else
                -- Traditional output: Loop and dump to standard Ashita log window
                -- for _, textLine in ipairs(reportLines) do ... print to chat ... end
            end

            e.blocked = true
        end
    end

    Architectural Perks for This System:

    Zero FPS Impact: Because io.open and file:write run synchronously inside a single frame execution chunk, keeping the file open only for the duration
    of a manual slash command run ensures it finishes in microseconds without dropping frame-rates during active play.

    True HTML Scaling: Wrapping layout steps sequentially via <div class='log-line'> ensures that if a player views the output on an external tablet or
    browser window layout, text wraps natively without formatting collapses.

    me: I've created an equivalency table mapping on screen colors to html color tags. At the moment I'm not doing anything fancy, just trying to replicate
    what goes to the screen. Definitely using div's and span's I can make the display look cleaner. For now though I am only interested in something simple

    Keeping it simple first is the smartest engineering approach. Building an equivalency table to translate your on-screen colors directly to standard hex
    codes gives you a perfect 1:1 match with minimal overhead.

    Since you are focusing on a straightforward replication, you don't need complex CSS layouts. You can achieve a completely readable, clean result just by
    outputting raw, sequential text lines wrapped in basic <div> blocks, using <span> for your color shifts.

    Here is a streamlined way to structure that simple string-translation loop using your equivalency table before writing the results to your file.

    Step 1: Your Color Equivalency Table

    You can structure your table to map your custom screen tokens (whether they are Ashita chat codes, custom numbers, or text labels) directly to their
    corresponding HTML hex colors.

    -- Simple color translation map
    local color_to_hex = {
        ["white"]  = "#ffffff",
        ["green"]  = "#22ff22", -- Accessible
        ["red"]    = "#ff3333", -- Locked / Excluded
        ["yellow"] = "#ffaa00", -- Native block
        ["gray"]   = "#888888", -- Location note
        -- Add whatever markers or codes you are currently using for screen output
    }

    Step 2: A Lightweight Streamlined Exporter

    Instead of dealing with advanced document containers, this function writes out a basic HTML header to keep the background dark (so white text doesn't
    blind you) and loops straight through your pre-formatted string lines.

    local function writeSimpleHTML(filename, linesTable)
        local installPath = AshitaCore:GetInstallPath()
        if not string.match(installPath, "[/\\]$") then
            installPath = installPath .. "/"
        end

        local fullPath = string.format("%sconfig/addons/your_addon/%s.html", installPath, filename)

        local file, err = io.open(fullPath, "w")
        if not file then
            print("[MobDB Error] Could not write file: " .. tostring(err))
            return false
        end

        -- Write bare-minimum styling just to keep it looking like a standard log terminal
        file:write("<html>\n<body style='background-color:#111; color:#fff; font-family:monospace; padding:15px;'>\n")
        file:write("<h3>Gear Inspection Report - " .. os.date("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S") .. "</h3>\n")
        file:write("<hr style='border:1px solid #333;'>\n")

        -- Dump lines sequentially
        for _, line in ipairs(linesTable) do
            file:write("    <div style='margin-bottom:4px;'>" .. line .. "</div>\n")
        end

        file:write("</body>\n</html>")
        file:close()
        return true
    end

    Step 3: Feeding the Exporter Natively

    When your command engine processes your inspect request, you can generate your HTML layout strings inline. If your function already knows what
    color text to pass to the screen, you just feed those same strings to your <span> generator:

    -- Helper to wrap chunks cleanly
    local function wrapColor(text, colorKey)
        local hex = color_to_hex[colorKey] or "#ffffff"
        return string.format("<span style='color:%s;'>%s</span>", hex, text)
    end

    -- Inside your inspect logic loop:
    local htmlLines = {}

    -- Just replicate what goes to the screen line-by-line
    table.insert(htmlLines, wrapColor("Main Slot: ", "white") .. wrapColor("Kraken Club", "green") .. wrapColor(" [Wardrobe 1]", "gray"))
    table.insert(htmlLines, wrapColor("Sub Slot:  ", "white") .. wrapColor("LOCKED BY SCRIPT", "red"))

    -- Write it out if the user requested the file flag
    writeSimpleHTML("gear_inspect", htmlLines)

    Why this fits your immediate goals perfectly:

    True 1:1 Match: Because you use the exact same logic flow to build htmlLines as you do for your chat screen logs, your file output will
    perfectly mirror what you see in the game window.

    No Tag Bloat: Using basic block elements (<div>) for rows ensures that lines naturally break and stack vertically without needing complex
    positioning tricks.

    Low Maintenance: You can easily add, change, or tweak colors inside your color_to_hex table at any point without changing a single line
    of file-writing logic.

    me: the file name is either generate based on the day and what report is being saved or the user specifies a name. If the file already
    exists I want to append the new lines to it. (This means that the </body> tag has to be removed from the existing file before appending
    the new lines.) I also want to support the user indicating that an existing file should just be overwritten

    To handle appending data to an existing HTML file (which requires rolling back the closing </body></html> tags) versus overwriting it
    fresh, we can use standard Lua file I/O modes ("w" for overwrite, "r+" for appending/modifying in place).

    local function saveHTMLReport(filename, linesTable, appendMode)
    local installPath = AshitaCore:GetInstallPath()
    if not string.match(installPath, "[/\\]$") then
        installPath = installPath .. "/"
    end

    local fullPath = string.format("%sconfig/addons/your_addon/%s.html", installPath, filename)

        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        -- SCENARIO 1: APPEND MODE (File must exist, we strip tags, and append)
        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        if appendMode and ashita.fs.exists(fullPath) then
            -- Open in read/write update mode
            local file, err = io.open(fullPath, "r+")
            if not file then return false, err end

            -- Read the entire file content into memory to find the end tags
            local content = file:read("*all")

            -- Strip out closing body/html tags case-insensitively using Lua patterns
            content = string.gsub(content, "</[Bb][Oo][Dd][Yy]>", "")
            content = string.gsub(content, "</[Hh][Tt][Mm][Ll]>", "")

            -- Reset file pointer to the absolute beginning, truncate old data,
            -- and write the stripped contents back
            file:seek("set", 0)
            file:write(content)

            -- Add a visual separator for the new append section
            file:write(string.format("<br><h4 style='color:#888;'>Appended Report: %s</h4>\n", os.date("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")))
            file:write("<hr style='border:1px solid #222;'>\n")

            -- Append the new log lines
            for _, line in ipairs(linesTable) do
                file:write("    <div style='margin-bottom:4px;'>" .. line .. "</div>\n")
            end

            -- Seal the HTML structure cleanly back up
            file:write("</body>\n</html>")
            file:close()
            return true
        end

        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        -- SCENARIO 2: OVERWRITE MODE (Or fallback if file didn't exist yet)
        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        local file, err = io.open(fullPath, "w") -- "w" natively blanks the file out
        if not file then return false, err end

        file:write("<html>\n<body style='background-color:#111; color:#fff; font-family:monospace; padding:15px;'>\n")
        file:write("<h3>Gear Inspection Report - " .. os.date("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S") .. "</h3>\n")
        file:write("<hr style='border:1px solid #333;'>\n")

        for _, line in ipairs(linesTable) do
            file:write("    <div style='margin-bottom:4px;'>" .. line .. "</div>\n")
        end

        file:write("</body>\n</html>")
        file:close()
        return true
    end

    To handle appending data to an existing HTML file (which requires rolling back the closing </body></html> tags) versus overwriting it
    fresh, we can use standard Lua file I/O modes ("w" for overwrite, "r+" for appending/modifying in place).

    The Complete Cross-Platform File Handler

    local function saveHTMLReport(filename, linesTable, appendMode)
        local installPath = AshitaCore:GetInstallPath()
        if not string.match(installPath, "[/\\]$") then
            installPath = installPath .. "/"
        end

        local fullPath = string.format("%sconfig/addons/your_addon/%s.html", installPath, filename)

        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        -- SCENARIO 1: APPEND MODE (File must exist, we strip tags, and append)
        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        if appendMode and ashita.fs.exists(fullPath) then
            -- Open in read/write update mode
            local file, err = io.open(fullPath, "r+")
            if not file then return false, err end

            -- Read the entire file content into memory to find the end tags
            local content = file:read("*all")

            -- Strip out closing body/html tags case-insensitively using Lua patterns
            content = string.gsub(content, "</[Bb][Oo][Dd][Yy]>", "")
            content = string.gsub(content, "</[Hh][Tt][Mm][Ll]>", "")

            -- Reset file pointer to the absolute beginning, truncate old data,
            -- and write the stripped contents back
            file:seek("set", 0)
            file:write(content)

            -- Add a visual separator for the new append section
            file:write(string.format("<br><h4 style='color:#888;'>Appended Report: %s</h4>\n", os.date("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")))
            file:write("<hr style='border:1px solid #222;'>\n")

            -- Append the new log lines
            for _, line in ipairs(linesTable) do
                file:write("    <div style='margin-bottom:4px;'>" .. line .. "</div>\n")
            end

            -- Seal the HTML structure cleanly back up
            file:write("</body>\n</html>")
            file:close()
            return true
        end

        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        -- SCENARIO 2: OVERWRITE MODE (Or fallback if file didn't exist yet)
        -- -------------------------------------------------------------------------
        local file, err = io.open(fullPath, "w") -- "w" natively blanks the file out
        if not file then return false, err end

        file:write("<html>\n<body style='background-color:#111; color:#fff; font-family:monospace; padding:15px;'>\n")
        file:write("<h3>Gear Inspection Report - " .. os.date("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S") .. "</h3>\n")
        file:write("<hr style='border:1px solid #333;'>\n")

        for _, line in ipairs(linesTable) do
            file:write("    <div style='margin-bottom:4px;'>" .. line .. "</div>\n")
        end

        file:write("</body>\n</html>")
        file:close()
        return true
    end

    How to use this inside your code later:

    When processing your slash command args, you can just flag the boolean parameter:

    -- Examples:
    saveHTMLReport("Mon_Report", htmlLines, false) -- Overwrites fresh
    saveHTMLReport("MyLogFile", htmlLines, true)   -- Appends cleanly onto the end
